Chapter 2 · Inputs

Inside the inputs

The 364 legal terms, the 100 control words, the categorical probes, the antonym pairs.

Look inside the inputs

The dashboard so far has shown what the language models produced: distance maps, agreement scores, axis rankings, probe curves. This page is the other side — what the models received. The 364 legal terms with their real ordinance contexts, the 100 control words, the five categorical probes with their templated sentences, and the ten antonym pairs that built each of the six value axes. Every entry is verbatim from the input snapshots that §2.1, §2.3, §3.1.4 and §3.2.1 of the thesis describe.

The 364 legal terms — by domain, with real ordinance contexts

Every term is bilingual (English / Chinese), labelled with the domain into which §2.1 of the thesis sorts it and with the divisions of the Hong Kong Department of Justice that maintain it. Click a term to reveal up to two real passages from the Hong Kong ordinances that contain it (one English-side, one Chinese-side, when available), prefixed with the Cap. and section references.

The 100 everyday-language control terms

The control pool grounds the §3.1.1 legal-versus-control diagnostic and the §3.1.3 control-pool subtraction discussed on the Robustness page. The terms are drawn from the Swadesh basic-vocabulary list: pronouns, deictics, basic common nouns. They have no Hong Kong ordinance attestation by design — the only comparable reading the experiment runs on them is the bare encoding.

Categorical probes — eleven categories and five paraphrase templates per test

The §3.1.4 probes test whether the geometry preserves a doctrinally ordered sequence and places the largest gap at the legislatively expected break. For each test, the inputs are eleven category words in a defensible order plus five sentence templates that hold the syntactic frame constant while the category word varies. The template slot is shown as {category}. The doctrinally expected break is highlighted on the category row.

Age and criminal imputability (positive · borderline)

HK Cap. 226 s. 3 — conclusive doli incapax below age 10; rebuttable presumption 10-14
EN: 1.infant  →  2.toddler  →  3.young child  →  4.child  →  5.minor  →  6.juvenile  →  7.teenager  →  8.young adult  →  9.adult  →  10.middle-aged adult  →  11.elderly person
ZH: 1.嬰兒  →  2.幼兒  →  3.年幼兒童  →  4.兒童  →  5.未成年人  →  6.少年  →  7.青少年  →  8.青年  →  9.成年人  →  10.中年人  →  11.老年人
EN templates:
  1. the accused was an {category} when the act was committed
  2. an {category} committed the act in question
  3. at the time of the offence, the defendant was an {category}
  4. the perpetrator, an {category}, carried out the unlawful act
  5. the unlawful act was committed by an {category}
ZH templates:
  1. 犯案時，被告人是一名{category}
  2. 一名{category}實施了該違法行為
  3. 案發時，被告人為一名{category}
  4. 行為人是一名{category}
  5. 該違法行為由一名{category}所實施

Contract value (negative control) (negative control)

EN: 1.symbolic  →  2.nominal  →  3.token  →  4.small  →  5.modest  →  6.moderate  →  7.substantial  →  8.considerable  →  9.large  →  10.very large  →  11.massive
ZH: 1.象徵性  →  2.名義上  →  3.微不足道  →  4.小額  →  5.適度  →  6.中等  →  7.可觀  →  8.相當  →  9.巨大  →  10.極巨  →  11.龐大
EN templates:
  1. an oral agreement for the sale of land for a {category} consideration was entered into between the parties
  2. the parties orally agreed to sell land for a {category} sum
  3. an oral land sale contract was concluded for a {category} price
  4. the parties entered an oral contract for the sale of land at a {category} consideration
  5. an oral land transaction was made for a {category} amount
ZH templates:
  1. 雙方就以{category}代價買賣土地訂立口頭協議
  2. 雙方以{category}價格口頭約定買賣土地
  3. 就以{category}金額出售土地，雙方訂立口頭合約
  4. 當事人以{category}代價口頭達成土地買賣協議
  5. 土地買賣雙方以{category}價金口頭成交

Age and contractual capacity (positive)

HK age of majority at 18 (Cap. 410 s. 2)
EN: 1.infant  →  2.toddler  →  3.young child  →  4.child  →  5.minor  →  6.juvenile  →  7.teenager  →  8.young adult  →  9.adult  →  10.middle-aged adult  →  11.elderly person
ZH: 1.嬰兒  →  2.幼兒  →  3.年幼兒童  →  4.兒童  →  5.未成年人  →  6.少年  →  7.青少年  →  8.青年  →  9.成年人  →  10.中年人  →  11.老年人
EN templates:
  1. a contract for the sale of property was entered into by an {category}
  2. an {category} signed a contract to purchase real estate
  3. a property sale agreement was concluded by an {category}
  4. the contract for the sale of real estate was made by an {category}
  5. an {category} entered into an agreement to buy property
ZH templates:
  1. 由一名{category}訂立財產買賣合約
  2. 一名{category}簽訂購買房地產的合約
  3. 由一名{category}締結財產出售協議
  4. 房地產買賣合約由一名{category}訂立
  5. 一名{category}訂立購買財產的協議

Offence severity (summary / indictable divide) (positive)

HK summary / indictable procedural distinction (Magistrates Ordinance Cap. 227; Criminal Procedure Ordinance Cap. 221)
EN: 1.a bylaw contravention  →  2.a regulatory breach  →  3.a minor infraction  →  4.a petty offence  →  5.a minor offence  →  6.a simple offence  →  7.a summary offence  →  8.an indictable offence  →  9.a serious indictable offence  →  10.a grave offence  →  11.the most serious offence

EN templates:
  1. the defendant was charged with {category}
  2. the prosecution involves {category}
  3. the case before the court concerns {category}
  4. the court convicted the defendant of {category}
  5. the accused was found guilty of {category}
ZH templates:
  1. 被告人被控以{category}
  2. 控方所提出的指控涉及{category}
  3. 本案涉及{category}
  4. 法庭裁定被告人犯有{category}
  5. 被告人被裁定干犯{category}

Criminal disposal severity (determinate / indeterminate) (positive)

determinate prison sentence vs indeterminate detention / life imprisonment
EN: 1.a caution  →  2.a fine  →  3.a community service order  →  4.a probation order  →  5.a suspended sentence  →  6.a short custodial sentence  →  7.a determinate prison sentence  →  8.a long prison sentence  →  9.a very long prison sentence  →  10.an indeterminate detention  →  11.life imprisonment
ZH: 1.警告  →  2.罰款  →  3.社會服務令  →  4.感化令  →  5.緩刑  →  6.短期監禁  →  7.一般刑期  →  8.長期刑期  →  9.極長期刑期  →  10.不定期羈留  →  11.終身監禁
EN templates:
  1. the court sentenced the defendant to {category}
  2. the defendant received {category}
  3. the offender was given {category}
  4. the judge handed down {category}
  5. {category} was imposed on the defendant
ZH templates:
  1. 法庭判處被告人{category}
  2. 被告人獲判{category}
  3. 犯罪者被判{category}
  4. 法官對被告人判處{category}
  5. 被告人被處以{category}

Value axes — the ten antonym pairs that build each axis

Each of the six axes of §3.2 is built as the mean of ten antonym-pair difference vectors, normalised to unit length (Kozlowski, Taddy & Evans, 2019). The recipe is bilingual: each axis has an English version built from ten English pairs and a Chinese version built from ten Chinese pairs drawn from the doctrinal vocabulary of the corresponding tradition. The pairs are listed below in their snapshot order; the first entry is the positive pole, the second the negative pole.

Individual ↔ Collective (10 EN + 10 ZH pairs)

English antonym pairs

  1. individualvscollective
  2. personalvscommunal
  3. distinctvsshared
  4. autonomyvssolidarity
  5. libertyvsconformity
  6. independencevsinterdependence
  7. self-reliancevscooperation
  8. freedomvsobligation
  9. consentvsauthority
  10. voluntaryvsmandatory

Chinese antonym pairs

  1. 个人vs集体
  2. 单独vs共同
  3. 自主vs团结
  4. 自由vs服从
  5. 独立vs互助
  6. 自力更生vs合作
  7. 私心vs公心
  8. 同意vs权威
  9. 自愿vs强制
  10. 个体vs群体
Rights ↔ Duties (10 EN + 10 ZH pairs)

English antonym pairs

  1. rightvsduty
  2. entitlementvsobligation
  3. freedomvsresponsibility
  4. libertyvsconstraint
  5. permissionvsprohibition
  6. immunityvsliability
  7. privilegevsburden
  8. claimvsdebt
  9. autonomyvscompliance
  10. empowermentvssubjection
